The 6 fell across **5** distinct dependent nodes — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `cl-3.formulate-digit-dp`, `cl-3.formulate-automaton-dp`, `cl-4.divide-and-conquer-optimization` (**two** of the 6), `cl-4.knuth-yao-optimization`. They are not fixed by hand: **a longest-path depth makes `depth(dependent) >= 1 + depth(prerequisite)` by construction, so a stage that is a monotone function of that depth cannot inverse-order a drawn edge.** The inversions vanish as a *consequence* of the recomputation, which is why none is separately adjudicated. — **`entry_gate` IS RE-DERIVED ON ALL 179, NEVER CARRIED FORWARD.** It is a **documented pure function** of depth, not an independent field: `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84` and `:150-152` fix **`gate-a` iff PS-1 (depth 1)** and **`gate-c` for PS-2/PS-3/PS-4 (depth >= 2)**. The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. The 6 fell across **5** distinct dependent nodes — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `cl-3.formulate-digit-dp`, `cl-3.formulate-automaton-dp`, `cl-4.divide-and-conquer-optimization` (**two** of the 6), `cl-4.knuth-yao-optimization`. They are not fixed by hand: **a longest-path depth makes `depth(dependent) >= 1 + depth(prerequisite)` by construction, so a stage that is a monotone function of that depth cannot inverse-order a drawn edge.** The inversions vanish as a *consequence* of the recomputation, which is why none is separately adjudicated. — **`entry_gate` IS RE-DERIVED ON ALL 179, NEVER CARRIED FORWARD.** It is a **documented pure function** of depth, not an independent field: `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84` and `:150-152` fix **`gate-a` iff PS-1 (depth 1)** and **`gate-c` for PS-2/PS-3/PS-4 (depth >= 2)**. Only **one** node's gate actually moved — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `gate-a` → `gate-c`, the live example the charter cites at `../../C005-dp-map/nodes/cl-3-state-compression.yaml:273-276` (it read `PS-1`/`gate-a`/depth 1 against a rubric depth of **4**). **That the other 178 re-derivations reproduced the existing value is a RESULT, not a reason to have skipped them:** recomputing stages while leaving gates alone would have shipped **PS-4 nodes carrying `gate-a`** with every executable green — a new inconsistency introduced by the remediation itself, and the charter's own **High** "second false all-clear" risk. — **THE DISTRIBUTIONS, handed to SUB-15** (`F-943-3` stakes present-tense claims on exactly these, and **all of them moved**): `progression_stage` **PS-1 20 · PS-2 32 · PS-3 36 · PS-4 91 → PS-1 19 · PS-2 26 · PS-3 27 · PS-4 107**; `entry_gate` **`gate-a` 20 · `gate-c` 159 → `gate-a` 19 · `gate-c` 160**. The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. docs/research/C005-dp-map-schema/adjudication/01_schema-decision-ledger.md:235: | **`D-R3`** | **The map's `prerequisite_depth`, `progression_stage` and `entry_gate` are RECOMPUTED over the edge-complete (post-NEU-939) graph and rewritten to agree with it.** NEU-940 computed these against the **pre-NEU-939** graph, before it was edge-complete; the values have disagreed with the rubric ever since (`F-943-1`, HIGH, live, and — until C008 — unowned). **THE RE-ESTABLISHED COUNTS: 26 of 179** dimension-bearing nodes carried a wrong `prerequisite_depth`, **every one of them UNDER-reported, zero over-reported** (unanimity is the tell that one systematic cause, not 26 independent slips, is at work). The 6 fell across **5** distinct dependent nodes — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `cl-3.formulate-digit-dp`, `cl-3.formulate-automaton-dp`, `cl-4.divide-and-conquer-optimization` (**two** of the 6), `cl-4.knuth-yao-optimization`. They are not fixed by hand: **a longest-path depth makes `depth(dependent) >= 1 + depth(prerequisite)` by construction, so a stage that is a monotone function of that depth cannot inverse-order a drawn edge.** The inversions vanish as a *consequence* of the recomputation, which is why none is separately adjudicated. — **`entry_gate` IS RE-DERIVED ON ALL 179, NEVER CARRIED FORWARD.** It is a **documented pure function** of depth, not an independent field: `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84` and `:150-152` fix **`gate-a` iff PS-1 (depth 1)** and **`gate-c` for PS-2/PS-3/PS-4 (depth >= 2)**. The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. docs/research/C005-dp-map-schema/adjudication/01_schema-decision-ledger.md:235: | **`D-R3`** | **The map's `prerequisite_depth`, `progression_stage` and `entry_gate` are RECOMPUTED over the edge-complete (post-NEU-939) graph and rewritten to agree with it.** NEU-940 computed these against the **pre-NEU-939** graph, before it was edge-complete; the values have disagreed with the rubric ever since (`F-943-1`, HIGH, live, and — until C008 — unowned). **THE RE-ESTABLISHED COUNTS: 26 of 179** dimension-bearing nodes carried a wrong `prerequisite_depth`, **every one of them UNDER-reported, zero over-reported** (unanimity is the tell that one systematic cause, not 26 independent slips, is at work). Split **CL-3 17 · CL-4 9** (`cl-4-optimization/mainstream.yaml` 7, `cl-4-optimization/frontier.yaml` 2). **CL-1 and CL-2 carried ZERO wrong depths** and are not write targets. **16** nodes changed `progression_stage`, **all of them to `PS-4`** (13 CL-3, 3 CL-4-mainstream); **1** node changed `entry_gate`. **43 value lines changed in total (26 + 16 + 1), across exactly three node files.** — **THE STAGE INVERSIONS: 6 before, 0 after**, over the **25** cross-cluster edges the validator checks, concentrated **entirely** in the cross-cluster class (`intra_cluster` 293 checked / 0 inversions, both before and after). The 6 fell across **5** distinct dependent nodes — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `cl-3.formulate-digit-dp`, `cl-3.formulate-automaton-dp`, `cl-4.divide-and-conquer-optimization` (**two** of the 6), `cl-4.knuth-yao-optimization`. They are not fixed by hand: **a longest-path depth makes `depth(dependent) >= 1 + depth(prerequisite)` by construction, so a stage that is a monotone function of that depth cannot inverse-order a drawn edge.** The inversions vanish as a *consequence* of the recomputation, which is why none is separately adjudicated. — **`entry_gate` IS RE-DERIVED ON ALL 179, NEVER CARRIED FORWARD.** It is a **documented pure function** of depth, not an independent field: `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84` and `:150-152` fix **`gate-a` iff PS-1 (depth 1)** and **`gate-c` for PS-2/PS-3/PS-4 (depth >= 2)**. Only **one** node's gate actually moved — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `gate-a` → `gate-c`, the live example the charter cites at `../../C005-dp-map/nodes/cl-3-state-compression.yaml:273-276` (it read `PS-1`/`gate-a`/depth 1 against a rubric depth of **4**). **That the other 178 re-derivations reproduced the existing value is a RESULT, not a reason to have skipped them:** recomputing stages while leaving gates alone would have shipped **PS-4 nodes carrying `gate-a`** with every executable green — a new inconsistency introduced by the remediation itself, and the charter's own **High** "second false all-clear" risk. — **THE DISTRIBUTIONS, handed to SUB-15** (`F-943-3` stakes present-tense claims on exactly these, and **all of them moved**): `progression_stage` **PS-1 20 · PS-2 32 · PS-3 36 · PS-4 91 → PS-1 19 · PS-2 26 · PS-3 27 · PS-4 107**; `entry_gate` **`gate-a` 20 · `gate-c` 159 → `gate-a` 19 · `gate-c` 160**. Over the 179, **`gate-b`, `gate-d` and `gate-e` are instantiated by NO node** — **reported mechanically, not ruled on**: whether `F-943-3` closes, and on which limbs, is **SUB-15's** adjudication against this evidence, not this row's. — **THE 6 UNEXPLAINED VALUES — residual cause `unestablished`, and NOT absorbed into the corrected set.** The stated root cause (NEU-940 computing against the pre-939 graph) explains **20 of the 26**: those 20 declared depths *exactly equal* the depth recomputed with the NEU-939 cross-cluster edges removed. **The other 6 match NEITHER graph** — the `cl-4.matrix-exponentiation-dp` family: `cl-4.matrix-exponentiation-dp` (declared 4 \| rubric 5 \| pre-939 2), `cl-4.construct-transfer-matrix` (5 \| 6 \| 3), `cl-4.implement-modular-matrix-power` (6 \| 7 \| 4), `cl-4.recognize-matrix-exponentiable-dp` (5 \| 6 \| 3), `cl-4.smawk-application` (5 \| 6 \| 4), `cl-4.larsch-online-smawk-implementation` (6 \| 7 \| 5). **They are corrected exactly like the other 20 — the recomputation derives from the GRAPH, not from the diagnosis — but they are recorded here as UNEXPLAINED and must not be presented as explained by the stated cause.** (Distinct from the 6 *stage inversions*; a different set, not to be conflated.) The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. (2) **Repaired tree:** `ANNOTATION FINDINGS … 0 depth mismatches, 0 stage inversions`, `30/30 passed`, **exit 0**, both new limbs PASS, `cross_cluster checked 25 \| inversions 0`. (3) **Negative control A — the extended validator against the PRE-FIX node values:** **limb 2 FAILS**, `29/30`, **exit 1**, naming `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding rubric depth 4 => PS-4 requires gate-c, declares gate-a` — **the proof the new assertion can actually fail**, on the very tree that used to pass. (4) **Negative control B — a deliberately mismatched gate on the repaired tree:** **both limbs FAIL**, `28/30`, **exit 1**; reverted, and no mismatched gate ships. `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84`, `:120-123`, `:150-152` (the depth→stage and stage→gate rules this row applies rather than decides); `../../C005-dp-map-package/03_open-items-and-provisional-register.md:35-49` (the register's 26/6/5, re-established and found to agree); `../../C005-dp-map/manifest.yaml:6-11`, which makes this ledger the only channel through which a `settled` value changes | ✅ 4 recorded — **hand-patching the 26 named nodes** (*rejected: the register's list is a symptom inventory, not a derivation; patching it would leave every OTHER node's value unverified and would not re-derive the gates at all — the recomputation covers all 179 and is reproducible by anyone re-running the validator*); **recomputing the depths and stages but leaving `entry_gate` alone** (*rejected: this is the defect the slice came to fix, reproduced — it would ship PS-4 nodes carrying `gate-a`, invisible to every executable in the package, and would be filed as closed by SUB-15. `entry_gate` is a documented function of stage, so leaving it is not "no change", it is a NEW wrong value*); **making the new gate assertion a `console.log` annotation beside H1/H2** (*rejected: that is exactly `F3.6`'s finding — a pass that cannot fail proves nothing. The whole point of §H3 is that it is wired into `check()` and sets the exit code*); **silencing `package-completeness-gate.mjs`, which starts failing the moment this merges** (*rejected: it correctly asserts a state that has just stopped being true. It is a research script under `docs/research/`, not a repo CI check, so nothing in the pipeline goes red. Also revised if the **PS granularity itself** is re-bucketed by its owner (`01_progression-stages.md:100-106`, `PS-GRAN`, deferred to the creator's progression-plausibility review) — that re-buckets stages **without** touching `prerequisite_depth`, which is why the exact depth is stored per node. docs/research/C005-dp-map-package/02_authoring-requirements.md:184: | `entry_gate` | `gate-a` / `gate-c` | ⚠ **`F-943-3`: a deterministic function of `progression_stage` — NO independent information. Gates B and D instantiated by no node.** |
docs/research/C005-dp-map-package/03_open-items-and-provisional-register.md:110: | **Finding** | `entry_gate` is a **deterministic function** of `progression_stage` — `PS-1`↔`gate-a` (×20), `PS-2/3/4`↔`gate-c` (×159), **zero exceptions**. It therefore carries **no independent information** and **inherits `F-943-1` wholesale**. Gates **B and D are instantiated by no node**. |
docs/research/C005-dp-map-schema/adjudication/01_schema-decision-ledger.md:235: | **`D-R3`** | **The map's `prerequisite_depth`, `progression_stage` and `entry_gate` are RECOMPUTED over the edge-complete (post-NEU-939) graph and rewritten to agree with it.** NEU-940 computed these against the **pre-NEU-939** graph, before it was edge-complete; the values have disagreed with the rubric ever since (`F-943-1`, HIGH, live, and — until C008 — unowned). **THE RE-ESTABLISHED COUNTS: 26 of 179** dimension-bearing nodes carried a wrong `prerequisite_depth`, **every one of them UNDER-reported, zero over-reported** (unanimity is the tell that one systematic cause, not 26 independent slips, is at work). Split **CL-3 17 · CL-4 9** (`cl-4-optimization/mainstream.yaml` 7, `cl-4-optimization/frontier.yaml` 2). **CL-1 and CL-2 carried ZERO wrong depths** and are not write targets. **16** nodes changed `progression_stage`, **all of them to `PS-4`** (13 CL-3, 3 CL-4-mainstream); **1** node changed `entry_gate`. **43 value lines changed in total (26 + 16 + 1), across exactly three node files.** — **THE STAGE INVERSIONS: 6 before, 0 after**, over the **25** cross-cluster edges the validator checks, concentrated **entirely** in the cross-cluster class (`intra_cluster` 293 checked / 0 inversions, both before and after). The 6 fell across **5** distinct dependent nodes — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `cl-3.formulate-digit-dp`, `cl-3.formulate-automaton-dp`, `cl-4.divide-and-conquer-optimization` (**two** of the 6), `cl-4.knuth-yao-optimization`. They are not fixed by hand: **a longest-path depth makes `depth(dependent) >= 1 + depth(prerequisite)` by construction, so a stage that is a monotone function of that depth cannot inverse-order a drawn edge.** The inversions vanish as a *consequence* of the recomputation, which is why none is separately adjudicated. — **`entry_gate` IS RE-DERIVED ON ALL 179, NEVER CARRIED FORWARD.** It is a **documented pure function** of depth, not an independent field: `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84` and `:150-152` fix **`gate-a` iff PS-1 (depth 1)** and **`gate-c` for PS-2/PS-3/PS-4 (depth >= 2)**. Only **one** node's gate actually moved — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `gate-a` → `gate-c`, the live example the charter cites at `../../C005-dp-map/nodes/cl-3-state-compression.yaml:273-276` (it read `PS-1`/`gate-a`/depth 1 against a rubric depth of **4**). **That the other 178 re-derivations reproduced the existing value is a RESULT, not a reason to have skipped them:** recomputing stages while leaving gates alone would have shipped **PS-4 nodes carrying `gate-a`** with every executable green — a new inconsistency introduced by the remediation itself, and the charter's own **High** "second false all-clear" risk. — **THE DISTRIBUTIONS, handed to SUB-15** (`F-943-3` stakes present-tense claims on exactly these, and **all of them moved**): `progression_stage` **PS-1 20 · PS-2 32 · PS-3 36 · PS-4 91 → PS-1 19 · PS-2 26 · PS-3 27 · PS-4 107**; `entry_gate` **`gate-a` 20 · `gate-c` 159 → `gate-a` 19 · `gate-c` 160**. Over the 179, **`gate-b`, `gate-d` and `gate-e` are instantiated by NO node** — **reported mechanically, not ruled on**: whether `F-943-3` closes, and on which limbs, is **SUB-15's** adjudication against this evidence, not this row's. — **THE 6 UNEXPLAINED VALUES — residual cause `unestablished`, and NOT absorbed into the corrected set.** The stated root cause (NEU-940 computing against the pre-939 graph) explains **20 of the 26**: those 20 declared depths *exactly equal* the depth recomputed with the NEU-939 cross-cluster edges removed. **The other 6 match NEITHER graph** — the `cl-4.matrix-exponentiation-dp` family: `cl-4.matrix-exponentiation-dp` (declared 4 \| rubric 5 \| pre-939 2), `cl-4.construct-transfer-matrix` (5 \| 6 \| 3), `cl-4.implement-modular-matrix-power` (6 \| 7 \| 4), `cl-4.recognize-matrix-exponentiable-dp` (5 \| 6 \| 3), `cl-4.smawk-application` (5 \| 6 \| 4), `cl-4.larsch-online-smawk-implementation` (6 \| 7 \| 5). **They are corrected exactly like the other 20 — the recomputation derives from the GRAPH, not from the diagnosis — but they are recorded here as UNEXPLAINED and must not be presented as explained by the stated cause.** (Distinct from the 6 *stage inversions*; a different set, not to be conflated.) The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. (2) **Repaired tree:** `ANNOTATION FINDINGS … 0 depth mismatches, 0 stage inversions`, `30/30 passed`, **exit 0**, both new limbs PASS, `cross_cluster checked 25 \| inversions 0`. (3) **Negative control A — the extended validator against the PRE-FIX node values:** **limb 2 FAILS**, `29/30`, **exit 1**, naming `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding rubric depth 4 => PS-4 requires gate-c, declares gate-a` — **the proof the new assertion can actually fail**, on the very tree that used to pass. (4) **Negative control B — a deliberately mismatched gate on the repaired tree:** **both limbs FAIL**, `28/30`, **exit 1**; reverted, and no mismatched gate ships. `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84`, `:120-123`, `:150-152` (the depth→stage and stage→gate rules this row applies rather than decides); `../../C005-dp-map-package/03_open-items-and-provisional-register.md:35-49` (the register's 26/6/5, re-established and found to agree); `../../C005-dp-map/manifest.yaml:6-11`, which makes this ledger the only channel through which a `settled` value changes | ✅ 4 recorded — **hand-patching the 26 named nodes** (*rejected: the register's list is a symptom inventory, not a derivation; patching it would leave every OTHER node's value unverified and would not re-derive the gates at all — the recomputation covers all 179 and is reproducible by anyone re-running the validator*); **recomputing the depths and stages but leaving `entry_gate` alone** (*rejected: this is the defect the slice came to fix, reproduced — it would ship PS-4 nodes carrying `gate-a`, invisible to every executable in the package, and would be filed as closed by SUB-15. `entry_gate` is a documented function of stage, so leaving it is not "no change", it is a NEW wrong value*); **making the new gate assertion a `console.log` annotation beside H1/H2** (*rejected: that is exactly `F3.6`'s finding — a pass that cannot fail proves nothing. The whole point of §H3 is that it is wired into `check()` and sets the exit code*); **silencing `package-completeness-gate.mjs`, which starts failing the moment this merges** (*rejected: it correctly asserts a state that has just stopped being true. It is a research script under `docs/research/`, not a repo CI check, so nothing in the pipeline goes red. It is NOT edited by this slice — clearing it is SUB-15's, and doing it here would rebuild the oversized slice the F2.13 split exists to avoid*) | **The graph's edges change again** — any edge added, removed or re-targeted moves the longest-path depths, and depth → stage → gate must be recomputed together, in one change, through a new ledger row. §H3's limb 2 is the detector: it fails the build the moment a graph edit outruns a value recomputation. Also revised if the **PS granularity itself** is re-bucketed by its owner (`01_progression-stages.md:100-106`, `PS-GRAN`, deferred to the creator's progression-plausibility review) — that re-buckets stages **without** touching `prerequisite_depth`, which is why the exact depth is stored per node. Also revised if the **residual cause of the 6** is ever established. | **This task — NEU-954 (C008 SUB-14)** — for the recomputed values, the re-derived gates, the §H3 assertion and the counts handed downstream. **The RECORD half of `F-943-1` — `D-P2`, `AC-6-P`, both registers, the PG-7 block, the cross-reference generator and its view, and the package README — is SUB-15 (NEU-956)'s**, untouched here. **The PS-granularity flag stays NEU-940's owner's**, cited here, untouched here. |

docs/research/C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs:340: gateVsRubric.map((n) => `${n.id} rubric depth ${depth(n.id)} => ${stageForDepth(depth(n.id))} requires ` +
docs/research/C005-dp-map-package/01_cross-reference-view.md:5686: - MATERIAL AND BLOCKING — rerooting is TWO traversals of the same tree, so it meets JS-E1 twice, and the second pass is the harder conversion. The upward (post-order) pass has the same problem as any tree DP: depth 2e5 on a path-shaped tree against a ~1e4 JavaScript cap, no raisable limit. The downward pass is worse: it carries a value from parent to child, so the explicit-stack rewrite must push the parent's contribution onto the stack alongside the vertex, whereas the recursion carried it implicitly in the frame as an argument. The C++ default writes both passes as plain recursion and never confronts this. In JavaScript both passes must be rewritten with explicit stacks, and the downward one's per-frame payload must be made explicit — a genuine acquisition, at exactly the tree sizes rerooting is FOR (rerooting exists to avoid the O(n^2) of re-running the DP from every root, so it is only reached for when n is large — precisely where the JavaScript stack fails).
docs/research/C005-dp-map-package/01_cross-reference-view.md:5759: - MATERIAL AND BLOCKING — the flagship recursion finding of this audit. This node writes the post-order DFS, and the post-order DFS is exactly the shape JavaScript cannot run at competitive sizes. C++'s recursive post-order on a tree of n = 2e5 reaches depth 2e5 on a path-shaped tree; the default 8 MB stack absorbs it, and judges raise the limit for the cases that need more. JavaScript throws RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded at a depth on the order of 1e4 — an order of magnitude BELOW the input sizes this technique routinely meets — and a submitted program cannot raise the limit. So the recursive realization is not slow in JavaScript, it does not run at all on the standard adversarial input. The JavaScript realization must convert to an EXPLICIT STACK, which is materially harder than the recursion it replaces: post-order specifically requires either a two-phase visit marker or an iterator-position-per-node scheme, because a child's value must be folded into the parent AFTER all children are done. That conversion is a real acquisition a C++ learner never makes. A downstream agent that inherits the C++ default here ships an exercise that crashes.

The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. The 6 fell across **5** distinct dependent nodes — `cl-3.bitmask-state-encoding`, `cl-3.formulate-digit-dp`, `cl-3.formulate-automaton-dp`, `cl-4.divide-and-conquer-optimization` (**two** of the 6), `cl-4.knuth-yao-optimization`. They are not fixed by hand: **a longest-path depth makes `depth(dependent) >= 1 + depth(prerequisite)` by construction, so a stage that is a monotone function of that depth cannot inverse-order a drawn edge.** The inversions vanish as a *consequence* of the recomputation, which is why none is separately adjudicated. — **`entry_gate` IS RE-DERIVED ON ALL 179, NEVER CARRIED FORWARD.** It is a **documented pure function** of depth, not an independent field: `../../C005-dp-progression/01_progression-stages.md:78-84` and `:150-152` fix **`gate-a` iff PS-1 (depth 1)** and **`gate-c` for PS-2/PS-3/PS-4 (depth >= 2)**. The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`. The recomputation did **not** turn out to explain them. — **THE AGREEMENT NOW HAS AN EXECUTABLE HOME.** `../../C005-dp-map-integrity/validator/audit-graph-integrity.mjs` gains **§H3**: two `check()`ed, **build-fatal** `entry_gate` assertions over the 179 — **limb 1** (gate vs the node's own **declared** stage) and **limb 2** (gate vs the stage its **rubric-computed** depth implies, reusing the file's existing `depth()`; no second depth engine). **Both limbs were required, and limb 1 alone would have been a fraud:** pre-fix, **zero** nodes disagreed with their own declared stage, so a one-limb check **could not have failed** on the broken tree — reproducing precisely the un-failable weakness `F3.6` caught in the annotation findings. **The 8 frozen roots are bounded OUT** of both limbs: they carry `{}` and no `entry_gate`, sit at depth 0 by construction, and are neither swept into `gate-c` nor reported as violations. | **settled** | **The validator, run four ways, and the ANNOTATION FINDINGS line — never the `STRUCTURAL VERDICT`, which read `PASS` at exit 0 on the broken tree and would have been satisfied by doing nothing (`F3.6`).** (1) **Pre-fix baseline:** `28/28 passed`, `VERDICT: PASS`, **exit 0**, while reporting `26 depth mismatches, 6 stage inversions`.
